4
An Empirical Study

The analysis of economic and productivity convergence stands as a compelling area of inquiry within economics, offering an ideal application for our hierarchical panel data model as outlined in Section
2
. Specifically, starting with the seminal study by
Baumol (1986)
, numerous studies have been devoted to testing whether income or productivity of poorer economies are converging to those of richer economies. As
Durlauf (2003)
puts it, “
Few issues in empirical growth economics have received as much attention as the question of whether countries exhibit convergence
”. A main technique employed by these studies is ‘‘cross-country growth regressions”, where aggregate- or industry-level cross-country data are used to regress the average growth rates of per capita income (or labour productivity) over a long period on the initial level of income per capita (or labour productivity) and some additional control variables
1
1

1

In this study we follow
Rodrik (2013)
and define labour productivity of an industry as the industry’s real value added divided by its number of employees. As is well known, the value added of an industry, also referred to as gross domestic product (GDP)-by-industry, is the contribution of a private industry or government sector to overall GDP (The U.S. Bureau of Economic Analysis, 2006, available at
https://www.bea.gov/help/faq/184
). The definitions of labour productivity and value added, therefore, imply that an industry’s labour productivity can be considered as the industry’s “GDP per capita”, which in turn implies that neoclassical growth models predict not only conditional convergence in GDP per capita among economies but also industry-level convergence in labour productivity among economies.
. A negative and significant coefficient on the initial conditions is taken to be evident in favour of
\beta
-convergence. For excellent surveys of cross-country convergence studies, see
Durlauf (2003)
for example. Despite the increasing availability of disaggregated data at industry level, the hierarchical structure of these data, to the best of our knowledge, has rarely been explored.

4.1
Data

We begin our empirical analysis by introducing the data. For labor productivity (or real value added per employee), we follow
Rodrik (2013)
and use the dataset of UNIDO’s INDSTAT2, which provides data on value added (in nominal U.S dollars) and employment for 23 manufacturing industries at the ISIC two-digit level for a large number of countries. With this dataset, real value added can be computed by deflating the nominal value added by the US producer price index, and labor productivity can then be obtained by further dividing real value added by employment (i.e., number of employees). Growth in labor productivity is then measured as percentage change in labor productivity.

Our control variables include a wide range of factors that have been found to be important for assessing convergence. These include human capital (as measured by school enrollment), investment price, trade openness and terms of trade, institutions (measured by civil liberties), and government consumption share. We refer interested readers to
Sala-I-Martin et al. (2004)
for detailed definitions of a wide range of variables. A summary of the dataset is given in Table
A.1
and Table
A.2
. It should be noted that since geographical factors are usually time-invariant, they will be captured by the factor structures and thus are not included in the control variables. When measuring dependent and independent variables, we follow
Salimans (2012)
, and treat them differently. Specifically, the dependent variable is measured as a five-year moving average of economic growth, while all explanatory variables are measured at the beginning of each five year period. Due to data availability, our sample starts at 1963 and ends at 2018. For the same reason, the number of countries varies across industries.

4.2
Estimation Results

We now investigate conditional convergence for the manufacturing industries, which can be done by estimating equation (
2.2
) where all components (i.e., initial productivity, control variables and three types of factors) are included. The factor selection is achieved using the method that is described in Section
2.4
.

For the global factor, we identify one factor that affects the growth in labour productivity of each country in every manufacturing industry and it accounts for 22.50% of the variations in
y_{ijt}
. Panels A and B of Table
A.3
present the estimated numbers of industry and country factors. As evident from the table, the number of factors varies among industries and countries, ranging from 1 to 10. These factors collectively explain 56.65% of
y_{ijt}
’s variations. Overall, the factor structure can account for approximately 80% of the variations in
y_{ijt}
, indicating a superior goodness of fit for our model.

Figures
A.1
and
A.2
provide the boxplots of country-specific estimated coefficients for each explanatory variable across 23 industries. As evident in Figure
A.1
, the estimated partial effects from the initial productivity are generally negative with an average value of -0.157.
This suggests that when country characteristics are controlled, initial labour productivity is generally negatively related to the subsequent rate of growth in labour productivity. In other words, conditional convergence in labour productivity exists for the manufacturing industry. This finding is in line with that of
Rodrik (2013)
who, by applying a fixed effects panel data model to the UNIDO’s INDSTAT dataset, also finds conditional convergence in labour productivity for the total manufacturing industry. It is also consistent with the income convergence literature (
Sala-I-Martin et al., 2004
) that finds that once country characteristics are controlled for, the coefficient on initial income becomes negative and statistically significant. To show the disparity in conditional convergence of productivity among countries, we present the country-specific estimated partial effects for initial productivity within the food and beverages industry in Table
A.6
as an example. Despite the consistent negative estimates across all countries, there exists significant divergence in their magnitudes. For instance, coefficients for some countries such as Albania, Croatia, and Malawi exceed -0.25, whereas the effects are substantially lower in others like Pakistan and Peru, or even insignificant as in Jordan and Syria. This finding demonstrates the importance of employing heterogeneous panel data in the examination of productivity convergence. Moreover, such country-specific variations are also present in the estimates for other industries as well as the estimated partial effects for control variables.

In order to confirm our results regarding conditional convergence in labour productivity, we conduct two robustness checks. First, we re-estimate the model for the following two subperiods: 1973-2018 and 1983-2018. The results are provided in Figure
A.4
. It is evident that the estimated partial effects for initial productivity generally remain negative, confirming the conditional convergence of productivity. Secondly, we follow
Rodrik (2013)
and exclude OCED countries from our sample of countries. The results, which are also presented in Figure
A.4
, show that our findings of conditional convergence is very robust to the exclusion of OECD countries.

To summarize this section, we have examined the hypothesis of the conditional convergence of productivity for manufacturing industries across countries. The empirical results presented in this section suggest that there is strong and consistent evidence of convergence once factors that affect steady-state levels of labour productivity are controlled for. As comparison, we also examine the unconditional convergence of productivity by excluding the control variables. We provide the estimation results and discussion on such issue in Appendix A.

5
Conclusion

The hierarchy of large datasets has garnered considerable attention recently (e.g.,
Chen et al., 2022
,
Jin et al., 2024a
,
Jin et al. (2024b)
,
Zhang et al., 2023
, along with numerous real data examples collected in
Matyas et al., 2017
). In this study, we contribute to the literature by proposing a panel data regression model with three sets of latent factor structures, which, to the best of our knowledge, has not been extensively explored in the existing literature. Rather than consolidating factors from various nodes, as seen in the literature of distributed PCA (
Fan et al., 2019
), we propose an estimation method to recover the parameters of interest by peeling off factors layer by layer and across different blocks within certain layers. We establish estimation theory and asymptotic properties accordingly. Additionally, we present a bootstrap procedure to infer the parameters of interest, allowing for different types of cross-sectional dependence (CSD) and time-series autocorrelation (TSA) in the data generating process (DGP). Finally, we validate our theoretical findings using extensive simulated and real data examples. In our empirical study, we utilize data from manufacturing industries at the ISIC two-digit level to examine the twin hypotheses of conditional and unconditional convergence for manufacturing industries across countries.

Appendix A

This appendix provides the main tables listed in Section
4
as well as some extra estimation results for the empirical study discussed in Section
4
.

Tables
A.1
and
A.2
, and Figures
A.1
and
A.2
present some empirical results for conditional convergence. Figures
A.1
and
A.2
provide the boxplots of country-specific estimated coefficients for each explanatory variable across 23 industries. As shown in Figure
A.1
, the majority of the estimated coefficients associated with initial productivity exhibit values below zero, with a limited number of exceptions, while their distribution varies across industries. For the control variables, the estimated coefficients are distributed around zero and they exhibit more evident heterogeneity in different industries.

We then examine the unconditional convergence of labour productivity by excluding the control variables that are employed in Section
4
. We re-estimate the numbers of factors using the method proposed in Section
2.4
and report the results for industry- and country-specific factors in Table
A.4
. The values are ranging from 1 to 9 for different industries and countries. In total, the industry- and country-specific factors can account for 65.77% of
y_{ijt}
’s variations. In addition, we identify one global factor that can explains 27.88% of the variations in
y_{ijt}
. For the estimated coefficients for initial productivity, we present their distributions for each industry in Figure
A.3
. As can be seen from this figure, most estimated effects of initial productivity on the subsequent growth of productivity are negative and these manufacturing industries exhibit evident difference in the distributions. Furthermore, we calculate the average value of initial productivity’s effects using different samples in Table
A.5
. The results also indicate a negative relationship between the initial productivity and the subsequent growth rate, which is robust to various sample selections. Additionally, we report the country-specific estimates and the associated 95% confidence intervals for the initial productivity in the food and beverages industry in Table
A.5
. It is evident to see that the estimated effects are significant and negative for most countries.

To summarize, we can confirm that both unconditional and conditional convergences of productivity exist for most countries after controlling unrecoverable heterogeneity properly.

Appendix B

Appendix B is organized as follows. Appendix
B.1
provides two detailed numerical implementations; Appendix
B.2
proposes a Jackknife procedure of bias correction for the mean group estimators; Appendix
B.3
includes the necessary preliminary lemmas; Appendix
B.4
presents the detailed proofs.

B.1
Numerical Implementation

In this appendix, we provide two numerical implementations. The first one is about the steps used to calculate simulation results, while the second one is specifically about the procedure of Section
2.2
.

1. Implementation about Simulation

1.

We prescribe
d_{\max}=5
for instance. Let
\widehat{\ell}=d_{\max}
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}=d_{\max}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{L}
, and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\bullet}=d_{\max}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{N}
to obtain
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}
as in the second implementation of this section.

2.

Once receiving
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}
, we calculate
\widehat{\ell}
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}
, and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\bullet}
as in Section
2.4
.

3.

Using the estimates
\widehat{\ell}
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}
, and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\bullet}
, we obtain the estimate of
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}
again as in the second implementation of this section, i.e., an updated
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}
. Simultaneously, we also obtain the estimates
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}
which serve as the estimated
\mathbf{F}
,
\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}
and
\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}
respectively.

4.

With all estimates in hand, we implement the DWB procedure as in Section
2.2
to calculate the coverage rates for all
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}
’s. For simplicity, we adopt the Bartlett kernel, i.e.,
a(x)=1-|x|
if
|x|\leq 1
, and let the bandwidth be
m=\lfloor 1.75T^{1/3}\rfloor
.

2. Implementation about Section
2.2

1.

Randomly generate each element of
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}
from standard normal distribution, and conduct SVD decomposition to update
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}
and ensure
\frac{1}{T}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{I}
. Generate the elements of
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}
from standard normal distribution, and update
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}
via SVD decomposition for
\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ}
and
\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}
.

2.

Calculate
\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}
using
(\widehat{\mathbf{C}},\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ},\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet})
as in step (a) of (
2.13
).

3.

Based on
\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}
, update
(\widehat{\mathbf{C}},\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ},\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet})
as in steps (b)-(d) of (
2.13
).

4.

Calculate
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}
using
(\widehat{\mathbf{C}},\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ},\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet})
as in (
2.14
).

5.

Repeat steps (2)-(4) until reaching some convergence, say,
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{(n)}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{(n-1)}\|/\sqrt{LN}<\epsilon
, where
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}=(\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{11},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{LN})^{\top}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{(n)}
stands for the value of
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}
obtained in the
n^{th}
replication.

B.2
Bias Correction

In this appendix, we propose a Jackknife method to eliminate the bias in the CLT of mean group estimators
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot j}
. Since
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot j}
have similar structures, we only focus on the bias correction for
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}
.
Here, we introduce some new notation to reorganize the bias terms according to their probability orders. Specifically, we define the following mathematical symbols:

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,ij}^{\circ}=(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,1}^{\circ},\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,2,i}^{\circ},\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,3,j}^{\circ})
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,ij}^{\bullet}=(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,1}^{\bullet},\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,2,i}^{\bullet},\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,3,j}^{\bullet})
with
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,1}^{\circ}=\sum_{ij}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot})^{-1}
,

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,2,i}^{\circ}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,1}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ})^{-1}
,

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,3,j}^{\circ}=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,1}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}+\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\big)\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\bullet\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\bullet})^{-1}
,

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,1}^{\bullet}=\sum_{ij}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot})^{-1}
,

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,2,i}^{\bullet}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,1}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}+\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}\big)\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ})^{-1}
, and

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,3,j}^{\bullet}=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,1}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\bullet\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\bullet})^{-1}
.

Drawing upon the arguments used in the proof of Theorem
1
.(2), we can further demonstrate that

\displaystyle\sqrt{NT}(\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i\centerdot})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{NT}}{L}\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,1}+\sqrt{\frac{T}{N}}\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,2}+\sqrt{\frac{T}{L}}\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,3}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}+o_{P}(1),

(B.1)

where
\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,1}
,
\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,2}
and
\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,3}
denote the probability limits of the bias terms:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\plim_{L,N,T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{L}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{\ast\top}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{\ast}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,ij}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\ast},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\plim_{L,N,T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{N}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{\ast\top}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{\ast}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,ij}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\ast},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\plim_{L,N,T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sqrt{LN}}{T}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\Big(\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{\ast\top}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{\ast}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,ij}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\ast}+\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{\ast\top}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{\ast}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{F,ij}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\ast}\Big).

We develop a split-sample bias correction method based on equation (
B.1
). For notational simplicity, assume both
L
and
N
are even numbers. We can then split the sample into two halves along the
i
- and
j
-dimensions, respectively.
Specifically, define the sets
\mathcal{S}_{L/2,1}=\big\{i,1,\ldots,\frac{L}{2}\big\}
,
\mathcal{S}_{L/2,2}=\big\{i,\frac{L}{2}+1,\ldots,L\big\}
,
\mathcal{S}_{N/2,1}=\big\{1,\ldots,\frac{N}{2}\big\}
,
\mathcal{S}_{N/2,2}=\big\{\frac{N}{2}+1,\ldots,N\big\}
.
Using these sample splits, we construct four mean group estimators:
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot,1}
,
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot,2}
,
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot,3}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot,4}
using the observations indexed by
\mathcal{S}_{L/2,1}\times\mathcal{S}_{N/2,1}
,
\mathcal{S}_{L/2,2}\times\mathcal{S}_{N/2,1}
,
\mathcal{S}_{L/2,1}\times\mathcal{S}_{N/2,2}
and
\mathcal{S}_{L/2,2}\times\mathcal{S}_{N/2,2}
, respectively.
The average of these four estimators is denoted by
\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}
. Using similar arguments to those in the proof of Theorem
1
.(2), we can show that

\sqrt{NT}(\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i\centerdot})=2\frac{\sqrt{NT}}{L}\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,1}+2\sqrt{\frac{T}{N}}\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,2}+2\sqrt{\frac{T}{L}}\mathcal{A}_{i\centerdot,3}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}+o_{P}(1).

(B.2)

Based on these split-sample results, we define the bias-corrected estimator as:
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot,bc}=2\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}-\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}
. Substituting the expansions for
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}
and
\overline{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}
in (
B.1
) and (
B.2
), it follows that

\displaystyle\sqrt{NT}(\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot,bc}-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i\centerdot})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}+o_{P}(1).

This result shows that the bias correction procedure successfully removes the leading bias terms, leaving an estimator with an asymptotic normal distribution centered around the true parameter
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i\centerdot}
. In connection with (
B.57
) in the proof of Theorem
1
.(2), it further demonstrates that the Jackknife bias-corrected estimator achieves the same asymptotic distribution as the original estimator, but without the bias present in the latter.

B.3
Preliminary Lemmas

We first introduce some additional notation.

Parameters:
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i\centerdot}=(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{iN})^{\top}
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot j}=(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1j},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{Lj})^{\top}
;

Regressors:
\mathbf{X}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}=(\mathbf{X}_{i1\centerdot},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{iN\centerdot})
and
\mathbf{X}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}=(\mathbf{X}_{1j\centerdot},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{Lj\centerdot})
;

Errors:
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{d}=\diag\{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{1\centerdot\centerdot},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{L\centerdot\centerdot}\}
;

Loadings:
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}=(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top})^{\top}
,
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}=(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{11},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1N},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{L1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{LN})^{\top}
,

\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{d}^{\circ}=\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{1\centerdot}^{\circ},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{L\centerdot}^{\circ}\}
,
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ}=(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i1}^{\circ},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{iN}^{\circ})^{\top}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\bullet}=(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{1j}^{\bullet},\ldots,\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{Lj}^{\bullet})^{\top}
;

Some matrices:
\mathbf{P}_{ij}^{\circ\bullet}=2\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}+\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}
, and
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}=(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{1}^{\circ}},\ldots,\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{L}^{\circ}})^{\top}
.

We present some preliminary lemmas below, of which Lemmas
B.1
,
B.2
, and
B.3
are some generic results.

Lemma B.1
.

Suppose that
E\left[\xi_{t}\right]=0
, and
\{\xi_{t}\}
is strictly
\alpha
-mixing process with the
\alpha
-mixing coefficient

\displaystyle\alpha(t)=\sup_{A\in\mathcal{F}_{-\infty}^{0},B\in\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\infty}}|P(A)P(B)-P(AB)|

satisfying
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha(t)^{\delta/(4+\delta)}=O(1)
for some
\delta>0
and
E|\xi_{t}|^{2+\delta/2}<\infty
, where
\mathcal{F}_{-\infty}^{0}
and
\mathcal{F}_{t}^{\infty}
are the
\sigma
-algebras generated by
\{\xi_{s}:s\leq 0\}
and
\{\xi_{s}:s\geq t\}
, respectively. Then we have

E\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{t}\right|^{2+\delta^{\ast}/2}\leq\frac{C}{T^{1+\delta^{\ast}/4}}E|\xi_{t}|^{2+\delta^{\ast}/2},

where
C
is a constant and
0<\delta^{\ast}<\delta
.

Lemma B.2
.

Suppose that
E\left[\xi_{it}\right]=0
, and for
\forall i
,
\{\xi_{it}\}
is strictly
\alpha
-mixing process with the
\alpha
-mixing coefficient

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}(t)=\sup_{A\in\mathcal{F}_{i,-\infty}^{0},B\in\mathcal{F}_{i,t}^{\infty}}|P(A)P(B)-P(AB)|

satisfying
\max_{i}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{i}(t)^{\delta_{i}/(4+\delta_{i})}=O(1)
with
\delta_{i}>0
and
\max_{i}E|\xi_{it}|^{2+\delta_{i}/2}<\infty
, where
\mathcal{F}_{i,-\infty}^{0}
and
\mathcal{F}_{i,t}^{\infty}
are the
\sigma
-algebras generated by
\{\xi_{is}:s\leq 0\}
and
\{\xi_{is}:s\geq t\}
, respectively. Then we have for any given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta^{\ast}\in(0,\min_{i}\delta_{i})
such that

\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)=O\left(\frac{N}{T^{1+\delta^{\ast}/4}}\right).

Lemma
B.2
infers, even for the case
N\asymp T
or
N/T\to\infty
(for this lemma only,
N
and
T
are generic notations and could be different from those in the main model), we are still able to achieve
\max_{1\leq i\leq N}|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}|=o_{P}(1)
under rather minor conditions. Notably, Lemma
B.2
does not impose any restrictions on
i
dimension and on the tail behavior of
\xi_{it}
, so it permits
\{\xi_{it}\}
to be correlated cross-sectionally and to have heavy tail behavior.

Lemma B.3
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{E}
are
n\times n
symmetric matrices and that
\mathbf{Q}=(\mathbf{Q}_{1},\mathbf{Q}_{2})
, where
\mathbf{Q}_{1}
is
n\times r
and
\mathbf{Q}_{2}
is
n\times(n-r)
, is an orthogonal matrix such that
\text{span}(\mathbf{Q}_{1})
is an invariant subspace for
\mathbf{A}
. Decompose
\mathbf{Q}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Q}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{Q}
as
\mathbf{Q}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Q}=\diag(\mathbf{D}_{1},\mathbf{D}_{2})
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{Q}=\{\mathbf{E}_{ij}\}_{2\times 2}
. Let
\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{D}_{1},\mathbf{D}_{2})=\min_{\lambda_{1}\in\lambda(\mathbf{D}_{1}),\ \lambda_{2}\in\lambda(\mathbf{D}_{2})}|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|
. If
\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{D}_{1},\mathbf{D}_{2})>0
and
\|\mathbf{E}\|_{2}\leq\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{D}_{1},\mathbf{D}_{2})/5
, then there exists a
(n-r)\times r
matrix
\mathbf{P}
with
\|\mathbf{P}\|_{2}\leq 4\|\mathbf{E}_{21}\|_{2}/\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{D}_{1},\mathbf{D}_{2})
, such that the columns of
\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{0}=(\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{2}\mathbf{P})(\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}
define an orthonormal basis for a subspace that is invariant for
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{E}
.

Lemma B.4
.

Under Assumptions
1
.1-
1
.4, as
(L,N,T)\to(\infty,\infty,\infty)
,

1.

\sup_{\mathbf{C}_{\centerdot\centerdot}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{ij}^{\circ\bullet}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{LN\wedge T}+\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}^{2}}{NT}+\frac{\max_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\|_{2}^{2}}{LT}\right)
;

2.

\sup_{\mathbf{C}_{\centerdot\centerdot}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN\wedge T}}+\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}+\frac{\max_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{LT}}\right)
;

3.

\sup_{\mathbf{C}_{\centerdot\centerdot}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN\wedge T}}+\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}+\frac{\max_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{LT}}\right)
;

4.

\max_{i,j}\|\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}}-(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*\top})\|=o_{P}(1)
;

5.

\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{L\wedge T}\right)
;

6.

\sup_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{L\wedge T}\right)
;

7.

\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{N\wedge T}\right)
;

8.

\sup_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{N\wedge T}\right)
.

The following lemmas are necessary for Section
2.4
. To study the estimation of the numbers of factors, recall that we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}=\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\mathbf{Y}_{ij\centerdot}-\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})(\mathbf{Y}_{ij\centerdot}-\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})^{\top}.

Additionally, we let

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\mathbf{F}^{\top},

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}^{\sharp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}_{1}^{\sharp},\ldots,\mathbf{H}_{\ell}^{\sharp})\coloneqq\frac{1}{LNT}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\cdot\mathbf{F}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\sharp-1},

\displaystyle\lambda_{s}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for}\hskip 9.24994pts\in[\ell],

where
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\sharp}
is the
\ell\times\ell
principal diagonal of
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}
, and
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\sharp}
includes the eigenvectors corresponding to
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}^{\sharp}
.

Lemma B.5
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, as
(L,N,T)\to(\infty,\infty,\infty)
, we have the following results:

1.

|\widehat{\lambda}_{s}-\lambda_{s}|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\right)
for
s\in[\ell]
,

2.

\widehat{\lambda}_{s}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{LN}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{L\wedge N\wedge T}\right)
for
s=\ell+1,\ldots,d_{\max}
.

Building on Lemma
B.5
, we let

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}(\mathbf{Y}_{ij\centerdot}-\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})(\mathbf{Y}_{ij\centerdot}-\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}},

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LT}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\mathbf{Y}_{ij\centerdot}-\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})(\mathbf{Y}_{ij\centerdot}-\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}.

Additionally, we let

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{i}^{\circ}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top},

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{i}^{\circ\sharp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}_{i,1}^{\circ\sharp},\ldots,\mathbf{H}_{i,\ell_{i}^{\circ}}^{\circ\sharp})\coloneqq\frac{1}{NT}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ}\cdot\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ\sharp}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{i}^{\circ\sharp-1},

\displaystyle\lambda_{i,s}^{\circ}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{H}_{i,s}^{\circ\sharp\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{i}^{\circ}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\mathbf{H}_{i,s}^{\circ\sharp}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for}\hskip 9.24994pts\in[\ell_{i}^{\circ}],

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{j}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LT}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\bullet\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\bullet}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top},

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{j}^{\bullet\sharp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbf{H}_{j,1}^{\bullet\sharp},\ldots,\mathbf{H}_{j,\ell_{j}^{\bullet}}^{\bullet\sharp})\coloneqq\frac{1}{LT}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\bullet\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\bullet}\cdot\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet\sharp}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{j}^{\bullet\sharp-1},

\displaystyle\lambda_{j,s}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{H}_{j,s}^{\bullet\sharp\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{j}^{\bullet}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\mathbf{H}_{j,s}^{\bullet\sharp}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for}\hskip 9.24994pts\in[\ell_{j}^{\bullet}]

where
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{i}^{\circ\sharp}
is the
\ell_{i}^{\circ}\times\ell_{i}^{\circ}
principal diagonal of
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{i}^{\circ}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\circ\sharp}
includes the eigenvectors corresponding to
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{i}^{\circ\sharp}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{j}^{\bullet\sharp}
is the
\ell_{j}^{\bullet}\times\ell_{j}^{\bullet}
principal diagonal of
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{j}^{\bullet}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bullet\sharp}
includes the eigenvectors corresponding to
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{j}^{\bullet\sharp}
.

Let

\displaystyle\Delta^{\circ}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}\|+\frac{\sqrt{\log(LN)}}{\sqrt{N\wedge T}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}+\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}+\frac{\max_{i}\|\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}\|_{2}}{T},

\displaystyle\Delta^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}\|+\frac{\sqrt{\log(LN)}}{\sqrt{L\wedge T}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{\max_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{LT}}+\frac{\max_{j}\|\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}\|_{2}}{T}.

Apparently, we have
\Delta^{\circ}\to 0
and
\max_{j}\Delta^{\bullet}\to 0
in view of Assumption
1
.1 and Assumption
1
.3.

Lemma B.6
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, as
(L,N,T)\to(\infty,\infty,\infty)
, we have

1.

\max_{i}|\widehat{\lambda}_{i,s}^{\circ}-\lambda_{i,s}^{\circ}|=O_{P}(\Delta^{\circ})
for
s\in[\ell_{i}^{\circ}]
;

2.

\max_{i}\widehat{\lambda}_{i,s}^{\circ}=O_{P}(\Delta^{\circ 2})
for
s=\ell_{i}^{\circ}+1,\ldots,d_{\max}
;

3.

\max_{j}|\widehat{\lambda}_{j,s}^{\bullet}-\lambda_{j,s}^{\bullet}|=O_{P}(\Delta^{\bullet})
for
s\in[\ell_{j}^{\bullet}]
;

4.

\max_{j}\widehat{\lambda}_{j,s}^{\bullet}=O_{P}(\Delta^{\bullet 2})
for
s=\ell_{j}^{\bullet}+1,\ldots,d_{\max}
.

B.4
Proofs

Proof of Lemma
B.1
:

Lemma
B.1
holds immediately by Theorem 4.1 of
Shao and Yu (1996)
. Therefore, its proof is omitted.
\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
B.2
:

Write

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Pr\left(\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{E|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\xi_{it}|^{2+\delta_{i}^{\ast}/2}}{\varepsilon^{2+\delta_{i}^{\ast}/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{T^{1+\min_{i}\delta_{i}^{\ast}/4}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}E\left|\xi_{it}\right|^{2+\delta_{i}^{\ast}/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{N}{T^{1+\delta^{\ast}/4}},

where we let
\delta^{\ast}\coloneqq\min_{i}\delta_{i}^{\ast}
, the second inequality follows from Chebyshev’s inequality, and the third inequality holds by Lemma
B.1
.

The proof is therefore completed.
\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
B.3
:

The proof is given in Theorem 8.1.10 of
Golub and Van Loan (2013)
.
\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
B.4
:

In what follows, we repeatedly use the fact that
\tr(\mathbf{A})\leq\text{rank}(\mathbf{A})\cdot\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2}
, and shall not mention it again.

(1). By the definition of
\mathbf{P}_{ij}^{\circ\bullet}
defined in Appendix A, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{ij}^{\circ\bullet}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}.

We then consider the terms of the second line one by one.

For
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}
, write

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{1}{LNT}\tr\{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\,\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot\centerdot\centerdot}\}

(B.3)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{LN\wedge T}\right),

where we have used Assumption
1
.1.

For
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}
, write

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\tr\{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}^{2}}{NT},

where the second equality follows from Assumption
1
.1. By noting
i
and
j
are symmetric, we can similarly obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\bullet}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}^{\bullet}_{j}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}=O_{P}(1)\frac{\max_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\|_{2}^{2}}{LT}.

Putting everything together, the result follows.

(2). Recall that we have defined
\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}
in (
2.3
) and in Appendix A respectively. Write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}

(B.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}-\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}-\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}.

In what follows, we investigate the terms on the right hand side one by one. By Assumption
1
.1, we immediately obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN\wedge T}}\right).

(B.5)

Thus, we only need to consider three terms on the right hand side of (
B.4
).

We start from the term
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}
, which can be further decomposed as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}.

For
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}
, write

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{1}{LNT}|\tr\{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot\centerdot\centerdot}\}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\|\mathbf{F}\|_{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\sqrt{T}\cdot\sqrt{LN}\cdot\sqrt{LN\vee T}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN\wedge T}},

where
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}
is defined in Appendix A, and the second equality follows from Assumptions
1
.1-
1
.3.

For
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}
, write

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\right|

(B.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{1}{LNT}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{L}\tr\{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{1}{LNT}\left|\tr\{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{d}^{\circ\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\sqrt{L\vee T}\cdot\sqrt{N}\cdot\sqrt{LN\vee T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{\sqrt{(L\wedge T)(LN\wedge T)}},

where
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{d}^{\circ}
is defined in Appendix A, and the first inequality follows from Assumptions
1
.1 and
1
.3 and the following development:

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{d}^{\circ}\|_{2}=\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{d}^{\circ\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{d}^{\circ})}\leq\sqrt{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ}\|^{2}}=O_{P}(\sqrt{N}),

in which the second equality follows from Assumption
1
.2.

Similar to
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}
and by noting
i
and
j
are symmetric, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}\right|=O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{\sqrt{(N\wedge T)(LN\wedge T)}}.

Up to this point, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}\right|=O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN\wedge T}}.

(B.7)

Next, we investigate
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}
, which can also be further decomposed as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}.

For
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}
, write

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\frac{1}{LNT}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{L}\tr\{\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\frac{1}{LNT}\left|\tr\{\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{d}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\|\mathbf{F}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{d}\|_{2}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\sqrt{T}\cdot\sqrt{LN}\cdot\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}},

where
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{d}
are defined in Appendix A, and the second inequality follows from Assumptions
1
.1-
1
.3 and the following development:

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}\|_{2}=\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\right)}\leq\sqrt{L}.

(B.8)

For
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}
, we write

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\frac{1}{LNT}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{L}\tr\{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{NT}\sqrt{T}\cdot\sqrt{N}\cdot\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}},

where the first inequality follows from Assumptions
1
.1-
1
.3.

For
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}
, write

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\frac{1}{LNT}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{L}\tr\{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}^{\bullet}\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\bullet\top}\}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{NT}\sqrt{N\vee T}\cdot\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\|\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\bullet\top}\}\|_{2}\cdot\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\wedge T}}\cdot\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}},

where the first inequality follows from Assumptions
1
.1 and
1
.3, and the second inequality the following development:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\|\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\bullet\top}\}\|_{2}\leq\max_{j}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{i=1}^{L}\|\gamma_{ij}^{\bullet}\|\leq\max_{j}\frac{1}{\sqrt{L}}\|\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\bullet}\|=O_{P}(1)

in which we have used Assumption
1
.2.

Thus, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}\right|=O_{P}(1)\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}.

(B.9)

Similar to the study of
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}
and by noting
i
and
j
being symmetric, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\bullet}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}\right|=O_{P}(1)\frac{\max_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{LT}}.

(B.10)

According to (
B.4
), (
B.5
), (
B.7
), (
B.9
) and (
B.10
), we finally obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}_{\centerdot\centerdot}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN\wedge T}}+\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}+\frac{\max_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{LT}}\right).

(3). Write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}

(B.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}.

In what follows, we consider the terms on the right hand side of (
B.11
) one by one. By Assumption
1
.1, we immediately obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN\wedge T}}\right).

(B.12)

Next, we consider
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}
.

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}\right|

(B.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{1}{LNT}|\tr\{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{X}_{\centerdot\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\diag\{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot\centerdot\centerdot}\}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\|\mathbf{X}_{\centerdot\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\sqrt{LNT}\cdot\sqrt{LN\vee T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN\wedge T}},

where the first inequality follows from the fact that
\|\diag\{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\}\|_{2}<\infty
due to
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}
’s being fixed parameters, and the second inequality follows from Assumptions
1
.1 and
1
.4.

We then consider
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}
.

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}\right|

(B.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\circ}}\frac{1}{LNT}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{L}\tr\{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{X}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\diag\{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i\centerdot}^{\top}\}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{NT}\sqrt{NT}\cdot\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),

where
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i\centerdot}
is defined in Appendix A, the first inequality follows from Assumptions
1
.1 and
1
.4 and the fact that
\|\diag\{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i\centerdot}^{\top}\}\|_{2}<\infty
due to
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}
’s being fixed parameters.

Similar to the study for
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}
and by noting
i
and
j
being symmetric, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}^{\bullet}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}\right|=O_{P}(1)\frac{\max_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{LT}}.

(B.15)

According to (
B.11
), (
B.12
), (
B.13
), (
B.14
) and (
B.15
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}_{\centerdot\centerdot}}\left|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN\wedge T}}+\frac{\max_{i}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{NT}}+\frac{\max_{j}\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{LT}}\right).

(4). Write

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}\left\|\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*\top}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}\frac{1}{T}\left\|\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\left[(\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}/T)^{-1}-\mathbf{I}_{\ell+\ell_{i}^{\circ}+\ell_{j}^{\bullet}}\right]\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*\top}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{P}(1),

where the last step follows from Assumption
1
.3.

(5). Consider
\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}
, and write

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}}\tr\left\{\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{F}^{\circ}\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ}\}\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ\top}\}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\circ\top}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ}\}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{F}^{\circ}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{L\wedge T}

in which we have again used Assumptions
1
.2 and
1
.3 and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ\top}\}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\max_{i}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\|^{2}=O_{P}(1)

in which we have used Assumption
1
.2.

(6). Similar to the proof of (5), we can write

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tr\left\{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}^{\circ}\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ}\}\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ\top}\}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\circ\top}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ}\}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{F}^{\circ}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{L\wedge T}.

Equations (7)-(8) are obvious in view of the development of (5)-(6) as
i
and
j
dimensions are symmetric. The proof is now completed.
\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
1
:

(1). Recall that we have defined a few notations in Assumption
1
. Write

\displaystyle Q(\mathbf{b}_{\centerdot\centerdot},\mathbf{C}_{\centerdot\centerdot})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}.

By Lemma
B.4
and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it is easy to know that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}+O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\right),

where
\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}
is defined in (
2.3
) and
\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}
is defined in the beginning of Appendix A. Also, as shown in Remark
3
,

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|(\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*})-(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ},\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet})\|=o_{P}(1).

We now start our investigation, and consider

\displaystyle Q(\mathbf{b}_{\centerdot\centerdot},\mathbf{C}_{\centerdot\centerdot})-Q(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot},\mathbf{F}_{\centerdot\centerdot}),

where the definition of
\mathbf{F}_{\centerdot\centerdot}
is obvious. Note that the terms

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot},\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ},\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}

vanish automatically when taking difference. Thus, using Lemma
B.4
, we immediately obtain that

\displaystyle Q(\mathbf{b}_{\centerdot\centerdot},\mathbf{C}_{\centerdot\centerdot})-Q(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot},\mathbf{F}_{\centerdot\centerdot})

(B.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ},\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet})(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top})^{\top}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}+o_{P}(1).

We now focus on the right hand side of (
B.16
), and consider the following two cases:

\displaystyle\text{Case 1:}\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\mathbf{b}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|\leq c\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{Case 2:}\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\mathbf{b}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|>c,

where
c
is a large positive constant. Note that for Case 1, using Lemma
B.4
and Assumption
1
, equation (
B.16
) can further be simplified as follows:

\displaystyle Q(\mathbf{b}_{\centerdot\centerdot},\mathbf{C}_{\centerdot\centerdot})-Q(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot},\mathbf{F}_{\centerdot\centerdot})

(B.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{ij,1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{ij}^{\top}[E[\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*\top}]\otimes\mathbf{I}_{T}]\boldsymbol{\eta}_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{ij,2}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}+o_{P}(1),

where
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{ij}=\text{vec}(\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\mathbf{F})
, and the second equality follows from Assumption
1
.2. Following the development on page 1265 of
Bai (2009)
and using (
B.17
) and Assumption
1
.2, it is easy to see that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|=o_{P}(1)
if we show that Case 2 does not hold, which is exactly what we are about to do.

For Case 2, we write (
B.16
) as follows:

\displaystyle Q(\mathbf{b}_{\centerdot\centerdot},\mathbf{C}_{\centerdot\centerdot})-Q(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot},\mathbf{F}_{\centerdot\centerdot})

(B.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}[(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})+\boldsymbol{\theta}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{ij}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{ij}]

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ},\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet})(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top})^{\top}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle a_{0}c^{2}+\frac{1}{LN}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{B}_{ij}\boldsymbol{\theta}_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ},\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet})(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top})^{\top}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{ij}^{\dagger}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\circ}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}^{\bullet}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}+o_{P}(1),

where
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{ij}
and
\mathbf{B}_{ij}
are defined in the same fashion as that on page 1265 of
Bai (2009)
,
a_{0}
is a positive constant by Assumption
1
. Apparently, Case 2 cannot hold by comparing the right hand sides of (
B.16
) and (
B.18
). The proof of the first result is now completed.

(2). By (
2.12
), we write

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}=\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

(B.19)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}+\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{J}_{25},

where the definitions of
\mathbf{J}_{1}
to
\mathbf{J}_{25}
are self evident.

For
\mathbf{J}_{1}
, write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{J}_{1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\|\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{LN}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|^{2}\right),

where the first inequality follows from the triangle inequality and the fact that
\frac{1}{T}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{I}_{\ell}
, and the second inequality follows from Assumption
1
.4.

For
\mathbf{J}_{2}
, write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{J}_{2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\left\|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\left\{\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\|\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})\|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\left\{\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\|\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|\right),

where the second inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and third inequality follows from the development for
\mathbf{J}_{1}
and Assumptions
1
.2-
1
.4. Similarly, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{J}_{3}\|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|\right).

For
\mathbf{J}_{4}
, write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{J}_{4}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbf{X}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\diag\{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot j}^{\top}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot j}^{\top}\}\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ\top}\}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\circ\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\|\mathbf{F}^{\circ}\|_{2}\cdot\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|\mathbf{X}_{\centerdot j\centerdot}\diag\{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot j}^{\top}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot j}^{\top}\}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ\top}\}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\frac{1}{LNT}\sqrt{L\vee T}\cdot\sqrt{T}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|\cdot\sqrt{N}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge T}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|\right)

where the third inequality follows from the development of
\mathbf{J}_{1}
and
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|\diag\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ\top}\}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{P}(1)
as shown in the proof of Lemma
B.4
.

Similar to the development of
\mathbf{J}_{4}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{J}_{5}\|_{2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge T}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|\right).

Similar to
\mathbf{J}_{4}
and
\mathbf{J}_{5}
and by noting
i
and
j
being symmetric, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{J}_{6}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\wedge T}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{J}_{7}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\wedge T}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|\right).

Similar to the development of
\mathbf{J}_{2}
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{J}_{8}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{J}_{9}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|\right).

For
\mathbf{J}_{11}
, write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{J}_{11}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\|\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\left\{\frac{1}{LNT}\sum_{i,j}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq
