\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{\sqrt{L\vee T}}{\sqrt{L}}\max_{i,j}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}\|_{2}\right),

(B.48)

It further yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i_{2},j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},i_{1}j}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{i_{1}j}^{\bullet\top}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j,6,2}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}j}}(\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i_{1}j}\right\|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge T}}\max_{i,j}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}\|\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\|_{2}\right).

Analogously, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i_{2},j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},i_{1}j}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{i_{1}j}^{\bullet\top}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j,6,4}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}j}}(\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i_{1}j}\right\|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN\wedge T}}\max_{i,j}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}\|\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\|_{2}\right).

For the term with
\mathbf{W}_{j,6,3}
, by invoking the properties of
\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}
and
\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}j}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}j}}\mathbf{W}_{j,6,3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LT}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{L}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}j}}\Big(\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{\bullet-1}\Big)\boldsymbol{\phi}_{i_{2}j}^{\bullet}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i_{2}j}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{i_{2}j})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i_{2}j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i_{2},j}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},i_{1}j}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{i_{1}j}^{\bullet\top}\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{j,6,3}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}j}}(\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i_{1}j}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\max_{i,j}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}\|\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{\bullet-1}\right\|_{2}\right).

Given the established results for
\mathbf{W}_{j,6}
, we are ready to assert

\sum_{s_{2}=1,\neq 10}^{25}\mathcal{M}^{\ast}_{i,31,6s_{2}}=o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).

Analogously, we have
\sum_{s_{2}=1,\neq 10}^{25}\mathcal{M}^{\ast}_{i,31,7s_{2}}=o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)
as well.

Building on the results established for
\mathbf{W}_{j,8}
,
\cdots
,
\mathbf{W}_{j,12}
,
\mathbf{W}_{j,15}
,
\mathbf{W}_{j,16}
,
\mathbf{W}_{j,20}
,
\cdots
,
\mathbf{W}_{j,25}
in the equations from (
B.26
) to (
B.27
), we can obtain that
\sum_{s_{2}=1,\neq 10}^{25}\mathcal{M}^{\ast}_{i,31,s_{1}s_{2}}
is negligible, for
s_{1}=8,\ldots,12,15,16,20,\ldots,25
.

We then consider the remaining terms. For
\mathbf{W}_{j,14}
, it is clear to see that

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}j}}\mathbf{W}_{j,14}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LT}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{L}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}j}}\Big(\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{\bullet-1}\Big)\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i_{2}j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{i_{2}j}^{\top}\Big(\mathbf{F}-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\Big)^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet},

from which we can derive the order for
\mathcal{M}^{\ast}_{i,31,14s_{2}}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{2}=1,\neq 10}^{25}\mathcal{M}^{\ast}_{i,31,14s_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{\bullet-1}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{T}\left\|\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\right\|^{2}_{2}\right).

For
\mathbf{W}_{j,17}
,

\displaystyle\max_{j}\frac{1}{LT}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{L}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LT}\max_{j}\left\|\mathbf{F}^{\circ}\text{diag}\big\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ\top}\big\}\text{diag}\big\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ\top}\big\}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\circ\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{LT}\left\|\mathbf{F}^{\circ}\right\|_{2}^{2}\max_{j}\left\|\text{diag}\big\{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\circ\top}\big\}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{L\wedge T}\right).

which immediately leads to the desired order for
\mathcal{M}^{\ast}_{i,31,17s_{2}}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{2}=1,\neq 10}^{25}\mathcal{M}^{\ast}_{i,31,17s_{2}}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{L\wedge T}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\right).

Similarly, for the term with
\mathbf{W}_{j,19}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{2}=1,\neq 10}^{25}\mathcal{M}^{\ast}_{i,31,19s_{2}}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge T}\sqrt{N\wedge T}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\right).

Additionally, considering the arguments that are used in the proof of (
B.46
), we can readily obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathcal{M}_{ij,31}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1}=13,18}\sum_{s_{2}=1,\neq 10}^{25}\mathcal{M}^{\ast}_{i,31,s_{1}s_{2}}+o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)

(B.49)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1}=13,18}\sum_{s_{2}=12,14}\mathcal{M}^{\ast}_{i,31,s_{1}s_{2}}+o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathcal{R}_{F,j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}+o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),

where
\mathcal{R}_{F,j}^{\bullet}=\sum_{i=1}^{L}(\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}+\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\bullet\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot j}^{\bullet})^{-1}
, and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{\bullet\top}\mathcal{R}_{F,j}^{\bullet\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\right\|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{L\wedge N\wedge T}\right).

(B.50)

Using the arguments that are similar to those in the proofs of (
B.46
) and (
B.49
), we can also formulate the leading-order terms in
\mathcal{M}_{ij,22}
,
\mathcal{M}_{ij,33}
,
\mathcal{M}_{ij,12}
,
\mathcal{M}_{ij,21}
,
\mathcal{M}_{ij,13}

\mathcal{M}_{ij,23}
and
\mathcal{M}_{ij,32}
, and finally obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{i,1,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}+o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),

(B.51)

where
\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*}=(\mathcal{R}_{F},\mathcal{R}_{F,i}^{\circ},\mathcal{R}_{F,j}^{\bullet})
with
\mathcal{R}_{F,i}^{\circ}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}+\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ\top}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{i\centerdot}^{\circ})^{-1}
. In light of (
B.47
) and (
B.50
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}\right\|=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{L\wedge N\wedge T}\right).

(B.52)

We have finished the investigation of
\mathbf{D}_{i,1,1}
and we now proceed to study
\mathbf{D}_{i,1,2}
. Similar to the development of
B.51
, we only need to investigate the leading-order term in
\mathbf{D}_{i,1,2}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{i,1,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\big(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{ij}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\big)\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}+o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{i,1,2}^{\ast}+o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).

It is clear to see that

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}^{\ast}_{i,1,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{ij}^{-1}\big(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{ij}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}\big)\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*},

Noteworthily, since
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{ij}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}+o_{P}(1)
, Assumption
2
ensures that
\max_{j}\left\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{ij}^{-1}\right\|_{2}=(\min_{j}\lambda_{\rm min}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{ij}))^{-1}

=O_{P}(1)
, where
\lambda_{\rm min}(\cdot)
denotes the the minimum eigenvalue function.

In connection with (
B.31
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\big\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{ij}^{-1}\big(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{ij}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{T}\left\|\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\right\|^{2}_{2}\right).

(B.53)

Using similar arguments in the proof of (
B.47
), (
B.50
), and (
B.52
), we can also establish that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left\|\frac{1}{T}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}\right\|^{2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{(L\wedge N\wedge T)^{2}}\right).

(B.54)

By (
B.53
), (
B.54
) and directly applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can readily show that

\mathbf{D}^{\ast}_{i,1,2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{L\wedge N\wedge T}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\right\|_{2}\right).

(B.55)

By (
B.51
) and (
B.55
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{i,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\phi}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathcal{R}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}+o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).

We then proceed to study
\mathbf{D}_{i,2}
. Write

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{i,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\big(\mathbf{F}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\big)\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\big(\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\mathbf{F}_{i}^{\circ}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ}\mathbf{H}_{i}^{\circ-1}\big)\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\circ}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\big(\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}}\mathbf{F}_{j}^{\bullet}-\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{\bullet-1}\big)\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{\bullet}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\big(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Xi}}_{ij}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\big)\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{i,2,1}+\cdots+\mathbf{D}_{i,2,4}.

For
\mathbf{D}_{i,2,1}
, using arguments that are closely related to those in the proof of (
B.46
), we can readily obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{i,2,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}+o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)

(B.56)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}-\frac{1}{NT^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{NT^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\circ\top}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}-\frac{1}{NT^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\bullet\top}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}.

Using the
\alpha
-mixing conditions in Assumption
2
and the independence of
\mathbf{v}_{ijt}
with the factors and loadings, we can easily show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\right\|=o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{NT}\right).

For the second term in
\mathbf{D}_{i,2,1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT^{2}}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT^{2}}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\big(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}\big)\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\right\|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT^{2}}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathcal{R}_{F}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\wedge T}\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\big\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}\big\|_{2}\right)+o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),

where the last equality holds by the fact
\|\mathbf{V}_{i\centerdot\centerdot}\|_{2}=O_{P}(\sqrt{N\vee T})
and
\|\mathcal{R}_{F}\|_{2}=O_{P}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\Big)
. Using analogous arguments, we can also show that the third and fourth terms in
\mathbf{D}_{i,2,1}
are also negligible. Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{i,2,1}=o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{i,2,2}=o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),\,\mathbf{D}_{i,2,3}=o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),\,\mathbf{D}_{i,2,4}=o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).

Combining these results, we can conclude that
\mathbf{D}_{i,2}
is negligible. Additionally, since
\mathbf{D}_{i,3}
has a similar structure with
\mathbf{D}_{i,2}
, arguments that are analogous to those in the derivation of
\mathbf{D}_{i,2}
can be applied to show that
\mathbf{D}_{i,3}=o_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)
.

For
\mathbf{D}_{i,4}
, following the developments for
\mathbf{D}_{i,1}
,
\mathbf{D}_{i,2}
and
\mathbf{D}_{i,3}
, we only need to study its leading-order term:
\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}
.
Building on the
\alpha
-mixing conditions in Assumption
2
and the conditional independence between
\mathbf{v}_{ijt}
and
\varepsilon_{ijt}
, we can readily obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}+o_{P}(1)

(B.57)

\displaystyle\to_{D}

\displaystyle N(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{b},i\centerdot}),

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{b},i\centerdot}=\lim_{N,T\to\infty}\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij_{1}}^{-1}E[\mathbf{V}_{ij_{1}\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij_{1}\centerdot}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij_{2}\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{ij_{2}\centerdot}]\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij_{2}}^{-1}
and the asymptotic normality can be easily proved using the standard large block and small block technique (cf.,
Gao, 2007
).

(3) Since
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot j}
has a similar structure to
\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{i\centerdot}
, we can use analogous arguments to those in the proof of Theorem
1
.(2) to prove Theorem
1
.(3). Therefore, the detailed proofs are omitted here.

Proof of Theorem
2
:

In the following proofs, we denote
E^{\ast}[\cdot]
and
\text{Var}^{\ast}(\cdot)
as the expectation and variance conditional on the observed sample, respectively.
\|\cdot\|_{n}
and
\|\cdot\|^{\ast}_{n}
denote the norms associated with
n
:
\|\zeta\|_{n}=E\bigl[\|\zeta\|^{n}\bigr]^{1/n}
and
\|\zeta\|^{\ast}_{n}=E^{\ast}\bigl[\|\zeta\|^{n}\bigr]^{1/n}
, for any random variable
\zeta
and any positive number
n\geq 1
. Additionally, let
\mathcal{F}_{t}
and
E_{t}[\cdot]
be the sigma field generated by
\{\mathbf{v}_{ijs},\varepsilon_{ijs}\}_{s=t,t-1,\cdots,\,i\in[L],j\in[N]}
and the expectation conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{t}
, respectively.

Write

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}^{*}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}}_{ij\centerdot}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})

(B.58)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})

\displaystyle+\left(\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}(\mathbf{F}_{ij}^{*}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{ij}^{*}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})

\displaystyle+\left(\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}(\mathbf{X}_{ij}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{ij}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{ij})\circ\boldsymbol{\xi}).

We then investigate the terms on the right hand side of (
B.58
) one by one. For the first term, using analogous arguments in the proof of Theorem
1
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})+o_{P}(1).

In what follows, we show that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})
can generate the bootstrap distribution. We first show
\text{Var}^{\ast}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})\Bigr)=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},\varepsilon,ij}+o_{P}(1)
. Note that

\displaystyle\text{Var}^{\ast}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})\Bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s,t=1}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijs}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijs}E^{\ast}\left[\xi_{t}\xi_{s}\right]

(B.59)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s,t=1}^{T}a\Bigl(\frac{t-s}{m}\Bigr)\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijs}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijs}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijt}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}^{2}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=t+1}^{T}a\Bigl(\frac{t-s}{m}\Bigr)\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijs}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijs}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}a\Bigl(\frac{t-s}{m}\Bigr)\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijs}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijs}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,1}+\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}+\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,3}.

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
B.59
), it is clear to see that
\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,1}\rightarrow_{P}E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijt}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}^{2}]
. For the second term,

\displaystyle E[\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}]-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=t+1}^{T}E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijs}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijs}]

(B.60)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=t+1}^{T}\Bigl[a\Bigl(\frac{t-s}{m}\Bigr)-1\Bigr]E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijs}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijs}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\Bigl(1-\frac{t}{T}\Bigr)\Bigl[a\Bigl(\frac{t}{m}\Bigr)-1\Bigr]E[\mathbf{v}_{ij1}\mathbf{v}_{ij,1+t}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ij1}\varepsilon_{ij,1+t}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{d_{T}}\Bigl(1-\frac{t}{T}\Bigr)\Bigl[a\Bigl(\frac{t}{m}\Bigr)-a(0)\Bigr]E[\mathbf{v}_{ij1}\mathbf{v}_{ij,1+t}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ij1}\varepsilon_{ij,1+t}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=d_{T}+1}^{T-1}\Bigl(1-\frac{t}{T}\Bigr)\Bigl[a\Bigl(\frac{t}{m}\Bigr)-a(0)\Bigr]E[\mathbf{v}_{ij1}\mathbf{v}_{ij,1+t}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ij1}\varepsilon_{ij,1+t}],

where
d_{T}
is a positive integer that satisfies
d_{T}^{2}/m\to 0
and
d_{T}\rightarrow\infty
.

For the first term, by the Davydov’s inequality for
\alpha
-mixing processes (see
Bosq, 2012
, pp. 19-20
) and Assumption
2
,

\displaystyle\|E[\mathbf{v}_{ij1}\mathbf{v}_{ij,1+t}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ij1}\varepsilon_{ij,1+t}]\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\alpha_{ij}(t)^{\kappa_{ij}/(4+\kappa_{ij})}E[|\mathbf{v}_{ij1}|^{2+\kappa_{ij}/2}]^{\frac{4}{4+\kappa_{ij}}}E[|\varepsilon_{ij1}|^{2+\kappa_{ij}/2}]^{\frac{4}{4+\kappa_{ij}}}

(B.61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(1)\alpha_{ij}(t)^{\kappa_{ij}/(4+\kappa_{ij})}.

In addition, by the Lipschitz continuity of
a(w)
on
[-1,1]
, it is clear to see that the first term in (
B.60
) has the order of

O(d_{T}^{2}/m)
.

For the second term, (
B.61
) yields that

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\sum_{t=d_{T}+1}^{T-1}\Bigl(1-\frac{t}{T}\Bigr)\Bigl[a\Bigl(\frac{t}{m}\Bigr)-a(0)\Bigr]E[\mathbf{v}_{ij1}\mathbf{v}_{ij,1+t}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ij1}\varepsilon_{ij,1+t}]\Bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\sum_{t=d_{T}+1}^{T-1}\alpha_{ij}(t)^{\kappa_{ij}/(4+\kappa_{ij})}.

Assumption
2
ensures that
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\alpha_{ij}(t)^{\kappa_{ij}/(4+\kappa_{ij})}
and
\sum_{t=1}^{d_{T}}\alpha_{ij}(t)^{\kappa_{ij}/(4+\kappa_{ij})}
have the same limit as
T\rightarrow\infty
and
d_{T}\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore, the second term in (
B.60
) is also negligible. Then, we proceed to study the order of
\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}-E[\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}]
. Define
\kappa_{ij}^{\ast}
as

\displaystyle\kappa^{\ast}_{ij}=1/\Bigl(\frac{1}{2+\kappa_{ij}}+\frac{\kappa_{ij}}{2(4+\kappa_{ij})}\Bigr).

(B.62)

Simple algebra indicates
\kappa_{ij}^{\ast}>2
. Then, the following result holds for
\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}-E[\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}]
:

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}-E[\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}]\bigr\|_{\kappa_{ij}^{\ast}/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\biggl\|\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T-s}a\Bigl(\frac{s}{m}\Bigr)\bigl(\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}-E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}]\bigr)\biggr\|_{\kappa_{ij}^{\ast}/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}a\Bigl(\frac{s}{m}\Bigr)\biggl\|\sum_{t=1}^{T-s}\bigl(\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}-E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}]\bigr)\biggr\|_{\kappa_{ij}^{\ast}/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{m}a\Bigl(\frac{s}{m}\Bigr)\biggl\|\sum_{t=1}^{T-s}\bigl(\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}-E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}]\bigr)\biggr\|_{\kappa_{ij}^{\ast}/2}.

Moreover, by McLeish’s inequality for
\alpha
-mixing processes
(
McLeish, 1975
, see Lemma 2.1 of)
and Assumption
2
,

\displaystyle\left\|E_{t-t_{0}}\bigl[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}-E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}]\bigr]\right\|_{\kappa_{ij}^{\ast}/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 6\alpha_{ij}(t_{0})^{\kappa_{ij}/(4+\kappa_{ij})}\Bigl\|\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}-E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}]\Bigr\|_{(2+\kappa_{ij})/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 12\alpha_{ij}(t_{0})^{\kappa_{ij}/(4+\kappa_{ij})}\bigl\|\mathbf{v}_{ij1}\mathbf{v}_{ij,1+s}^{\top}\bigr\|_{(2+\kappa_{ij})/2}\bigl\|\varepsilon_{ij1}\varepsilon_{ij,1+s}\bigr\|_{(2+\kappa_{ij})/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 12\alpha_{ij}(t_{0})^{\kappa_{ij}/(4+\kappa_{ij})}\bigl\|\mathbf{v}_{ij1}\bigr\|_{2+\kappa_{ij}}\bigl\|\varepsilon_{ij1}\bigr\|_{2+\kappa_{ij}},

for any positive integer
t_{0}
. Together with Lemma A of
Hansen (1992)
, it gives

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T-s}\bigl(\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}-E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ij,t+s}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ij,t+s}]\bigr)\right\|_{\kappa^{\ast}_{ij}/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 36c_{\kappa,ij}\Bigl(\kappa^{\ast}_{ij}/(\kappa^{\ast}_{ij}-2)\Bigr)^{3/2}\sum_{t_{0}=1}^{\infty}\alpha_{ij}(t_{0})^{\kappa_{ij}/(4+\kappa_{ij})}\left(T-s\right)^{2/\underline{\kappa}_{ij}},

where
c_{\kappa,ij}=12\|\mathbf{v}_{ij1}\|_{2+\kappa_{ij}}\|\varepsilon_{ij1}\|_{2+\kappa_{ij}}
and
\underline{\kappa}_{ij}=\min(\kappa^{\ast}_{ij},4)
. It follows that

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}-E[\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}]\bigr\|_{\kappa^{\ast}_{ij}/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(mT^{2/\underline{\kappa}_{ij}-1})=o(1),

which immediately yields
\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}-E[\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}]=o_{P}(1)
. Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,2}-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=t+1}^{T}E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijs}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijs}]=o_{P}(1).

Analogously, for
\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,3}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}^{\ast}_{ij,3}-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}E[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijs}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijs}]=o_{P}(1).

In summary of these results, we have

\text{Var}^{\ast}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})\Bigr)=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},\varepsilon,ij}+o_{P}(1).

(B.63)

Since
\xi_{t}
is
m
-dependent time series, we can follow the Theorem 3.1 of
Shao (2010)
and adopt the large-block and small-block argument to prove the central limit theorem for
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})
conditional on the observed sample. Define
l_{T}
and
s_{T}
as the lengths for the large and small blocks and
k_{T}=\lfloor T/(l_{T}+s_{T})\rfloor
such that
l_{T},s_{T}\rightarrow\infty
and they satisfy
\frac{m}{s_{T}},\,\frac{k_{T}s_{T}}{T},\Bigl(\frac{ml_{T}}{T}\Bigr)^{1+\kappa_{ij}/4}k_{T}\rightarrow 0
.

For
k=1,\ldots,k_{T}
, we define a series of variables for the large and small blocks, respectively:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}=\sum_{t=(k-1)(l_{T}+s_{T})+1}^{jl_{T}+(k-1)s_{T}}\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijt}\xi_{t},\hskip 9.24994pt\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,2}=\sum_{t=jl_{T}+(k-1)s_{T}+1}^{j(l_{T}+s_{T})}\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijt}\xi_{t}.

Moreover, we define
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij,0}=\sum_{t=k_{T}(l_{T}+s_{T})+1}^{T}\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijt}\xi_{t}
to contain the reminder terms.

Without loss of generality, we assume
l_{T},s_{T}>m
. Then, it is clear to see that
\{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij1,1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{k_{T},1}\}
are independent conditional on the observed data, as are
\{\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij1,2},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk_{T},2}\}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}E\Biggl[E^{\ast}\Biggl[\Biggl(\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,2}\Biggr)\Biggl(\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,2}\Biggr)^{\top}\Biggr]\Biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}E\left[E^{\ast}\left[\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,2}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast\top}_{ijk,2}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}\sum_{t,s=kl_{T}+(k-1)s_{T}+1}^{k(l_{T}+s_{T})}E\left[E^{\ast}\left[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijs}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijs}\xi_{t}\xi_{s}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}\sum_{t=kl_{T}+(k-1)s_{T}+1}^{k(l_{T}+s_{T})}\sum_{s=-s_{T}+1}^{s_{T}-1}a\Bigl(\frac{s}{m}\Bigr)\left|E\left[\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\mathbf{v}_{ijs}^{\top}\varepsilon_{ijt}\varepsilon_{ijs}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(\frac{k_{T}s_{T}}{T}\right).

Under the condition
\frac{k_{T}s_{T}}{T}\rightarrow 0
, we have
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,2}=o_{P}(1)
. Analogously, we can show that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij,0}=o_{P}(1)
.

In what follows, we establish the asymptotic normality of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}
(conditional on the observed sample) using the Lindeberg CLT. Similarly to (
B.63
), we can also show that

\frac{1}{T}\text{Var}^{\ast}\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}\Bigr)=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},\varepsilon,ij}+o_{P}(1).

For any
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}E^{\ast}\bigl[\big\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}\bigr\|^{2}\cdot I(\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}\|\geq\epsilon\sqrt{T})\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}\{E^{\ast}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}\|^{2+\kappa_{ij}/2}\}^{\frac{4}{4+\kappa_{ij}}}\left\{\frac{E^{\ast}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}\|^{2+\kappa_{ij}/2}}{\epsilon^{2+\kappa_{ij}/2}T^{\frac{4+\kappa_{ij}}{4}}}\right\}^{\frac{\kappa_{ij}}{4+\kappa_{ij}}}

(B.64)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\kappa_{ij}/2}T^{1+\kappa_{ij}/4}}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}E^{\ast}\big\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}\big\|^{2+\kappa_{ij}/2}.

Without loss of generality, we now use the Rosenthal inequality to study the order of
\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij1,1}\|^{\ast}_{2+\kappa_{ij}/2}
. Noteworthily, Rosenthal inequality is designed for the independent random variables. Therefore, we consider the following decomposition for
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij1,1}
:
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij1,1}=\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij1,1k}
,
where

\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij1,1k}=\sum_{s=1}^{\lfloor(l_{T}-k)/(m+1)\rfloor}\mathbf{v}_{ij,k+(s-1)(m+1)}\varepsilon_{ij,k+(s-1)(m+1)}\xi_{k+(s-1)(m+1)}.

By this definition, it is clear to see that the elements involved in
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij1,1k}
are independent conditional on the observed sample. Directly applying the triangle inequality and Rosenthal inequality sequentially, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij1,1}\|^{\ast}_{2+\kappa_{ij}/2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ij1,1k}\|^{\ast}_{2+\kappa_{ij}/2}

(B.65)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}\Biggl[\Biggl\|\sum_{s=1}^{\lfloor(l_{T}-k)/(m+1)\rfloor}\bigl\|\mathbf{v}_{ij,k+(s-1)(m+1)}\bigr\|^{2}\varepsilon_{ij,k+(s-1)(m+1)}^{2}\xi^{2}_{k+(s-1)(m+1)}\Biggr\|^{\ast}_{1+\kappa_{ij}/4}\Biggr]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}\Biggl[\sum_{s=1}^{\lfloor(l_{T}-k)/(m+1)\rfloor}\bigl\|\mathbf{v}_{ij,k+(s-1)(m+1)}\bigr\|^{2}\varepsilon_{ij,k+(s-1)(m+1)}^{2}\Biggr]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(\sqrt{m})\Biggl[\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}\sum_{s=1}^{\lfloor(l_{T}-k)/(m+1)\rfloor}\bigl\|\mathbf{v}_{ij,k+(s-1)(m+1)}\bigr\|^{2}\varepsilon_{ij,k+(s-1)(m+1)}^{2}\Biggr]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(\sqrt{m})\Biggl[\sum_{t=1}^{l_{T}}\bigl\|\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\bigr\|^{2}\varepsilon_{ijt}^{2}\Biggr]^{1/2}.

By (
B.64
) and (
B.65
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}E\bigl[E^{\ast}\bigl[\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}\bigr\|^{2}\cdot I(\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}\bigr\|\geq\epsilon\sqrt{T})\bigr]\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\left(\frac{m}{T}\right)^{1+\kappa_{ij}/4}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}E\Biggl[\Biggl(\sum_{t=(k-1)(l_{T}+s_{T})+1}^{kl_{T}+(k-1)s_{T}}\bigl\|\mathbf{v}_{ijt}\bigr\|^{2}\varepsilon_{ijt}^{2}\Biggr)^{1+\kappa_{ij}/4}\Biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\Bigl(\Bigl(\frac{ml_{T}}{T}\Bigr)^{1+\kappa_{ij}/4}k_{T}\Bigr)=o(1),

which implies that
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{T}}E^{\ast}\bigl[\big\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}\bigr\|^{2}\cdot I(\|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\ast}_{ijk,1}\|\geq\epsilon\sqrt{T})\bigr]=o_{P}(1)
. In summary of the results that we have established, the Lindeberg condition can be satisfied and

\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{V}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{E}}_{ij\centerdot}\circ\boldsymbol{\xi})\rightarrow_{D^{\ast}}N(\mathbf{0},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},\varepsilon,ij}).

Additionally, using analogous arguments to those in the proof of Theorem
1
, we can readily obtain that
\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}^{\top}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{ij}}\mathbf{X}_{ij\centerdot}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{v},ij}^{-1}+o_{P}(1)
and the second and third terms in (
B.58
) are asymptotically negligible. Therefore, we are ready to complete the proof of Theorem
2
.
\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
B.5
:

(1). Consider the first
\ell
eigenvalues of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, and note that by PCA, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{V}}=\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}.

We focus on the first
\ell
columns of
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}
, which is denoted as
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\sharp}
in the description of above this lemma. By (
B.22
), we know that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\sharp}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}^{\sharp}\|_{2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\right).

(B.66)

where
\mathbf{H}^{\sharp}
is also defined in the description of above this lemma. By the development of
\mathbf{J}_{1}
to
\mathbf{J}_{25}
in Lemma
1
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\right).

(B.67)

In the context of this lemma, we have also defined
\widehat{\lambda}_{s}
and
\lambda_{s}
. These notations and results will be repeatedly used below.

Let’s now consider
\widehat{\lambda}_{s}-\lambda_{s}
, and write

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{s}-\lambda_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}+\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp})^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}+\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp})^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp})

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp})^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp})

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\sqrt{T}}(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp})^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{1}+2\mathbf{A}_{2}+\mathbf{A}_{3}+2\mathbf{A}_{4}+\mathbf{A}_{5},

where the definitions of
\mathbf{A}_{1}
to
\mathbf{A}_{5}
are obvious.

By (
B.66
) and (
B.67
), we can immediately conclude that
|\mathbf{A}_{1}|=o_{P}(|\mathbf{A}_{5}|)
,
|\mathbf{A}_{2}|=o_{P}(|\mathbf{A}_{5}|)
, and
|\mathbf{A}_{3}|=o_{P}(|\mathbf{A}_{4}|)
. Thus, we focus on
\mathbf{A}_{4}
and
\mathbf{A}_{5}
below. For
\mathbf{A}_{4}
, we write

\displaystyle|\mathbf{A}_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}-\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}\|_{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\right),

where the last equality follows from (
B.66
) and the fact that
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{2}=O_{P}(1)
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{\ell}^{\sharp}\|_{2}=O_{P}(1)
by the construction. For
\mathbf{A}_{5}
, write

\displaystyle|\mathbf{A}_{5}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp})^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}\right|\leq\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{T}\|\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\sharp}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\right),

where the last step follows from (
B.67
).

This concludes the proof of the first result of this lemma.

(2). To investigate the second result, we start the proof by introducing some notations. We denote
\mathbf{F}^{\perp}
as a
T\times(T-\ell)
matrix such that
\frac{1}{T}(\mathbf{F}^{\perp},\mathbf{F}\mathbf{R})^{\top}(\mathbf{F}^{\perp},\mathbf{F}\mathbf{R})=\mathbf{I}_{T}
, where
\mathbf{R}
is an
\ell\times\ell
rotation matrix. The matrices
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}^{\perp}
,
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{R}
,
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
, and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
correspond to
\mathbf{Q}_{1}
,
\mathbf{Q}_{2}
,
\mathbf{A}
, and
\mathbf{E}
of Lemma
B.3
. Thus, the counterpart of the matrix
\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{0}
becomes

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\perp}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\mathbf{F}^{\perp}+\mathbf{F}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{P})(\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2},

in which

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\text{sep}(0,\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{R})}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{2}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\right).

(B.68)

Moreover,
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\perp}
is an orthonormal basis for a subspace that is invariant for
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
. In addition, note that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\perp}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}^{\perp}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\left[(\mathbf{F}^{\perp}+\mathbf{F}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{P})-\mathbf{F}^{\perp}(\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{1/2}\right](\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\mathbf{F}^{\perp}\left[\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}-(\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{1/2}\right](\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\mathbf{F}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\left\|\left[\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}-(\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{1/2}\right](\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{-1/2}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O_{P}(1)\left\|\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}-(\mathbf{I}_{T-\ell}+\mathbf{P}^{\top}\mathbf{P})^{1/2}\right\|_{2}+O_{P}(1)\left\|\mathbf{P}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(\|\mathbf{P}\|_{2})=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\right),

where the last equality follows from (
B.68
).

Now, let
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}^{\perp}
and
\mathbf{F}_{s}^{\perp}
be the
s^{th}
columns of
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\perp}
and
\mathbf{F}^{\perp}
respectively. Since
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\perp}
is an orthonormal basis for a subspace that is invariant for
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, for
\ell=1,\ldots,T-\ell
we write

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{\ell+s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}^{\perp}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}_{s}^{\perp}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}_{s}^{\perp}\right)^{\top}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}+\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\left(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}^{\perp}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}_{s}^{\perp}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}_{s}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\perp}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}^{\perp}\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+2\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\perp}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}^{\perp}\right\|_{2}\cdot\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\mathbf{F}^{\perp}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\perp}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{F}^{\perp}\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{LN}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{L\wedge N\wedge T}\right).

The proof of the second result of this lemma is now completed.
\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
B.6
:

The proof is almost identical to Lemma
B.5
, so we omit the details.
\blacksquare

Proof of Theorem
3
:

Note that

\displaystyle\Pr(\widehat{\ell}=\ell,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\circ},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\bullet}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bullet})=\Pr(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\circ},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\bullet}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bullet}|\widehat{\ell}=\ell)\Pr(\widehat{\ell}=\ell).

Therefore, in what follows, we first show that
\Pr(\widehat{\ell}=\ell)\to 1
, and then prove that
\Pr(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\circ},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\bullet}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bullet}|\widehat{\ell}=\ell)\to 1
in the second step.

Step 1. First, consider the case when
\ell=0
. By Lemma
B.5
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{\ell+s}=\widehat{\lambda}_{s}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{LN}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{L\wedge N\wedge T}}\right),

for
s=1,\ldots,d_{\max}
, which is less than
\omega
with a probability approaching one. By the construction of the mock eigenvalue, we immediately obtain that
\Pr(\ell=0)\to 1
.

Next, we consider the case with
\ell>0
. Note that for
s=1,\ldots,\ell
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{H}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{F}\cdot\frac{1}{LN}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\cdot\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}_{s}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{F}\cdot\frac{1}{LN}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\cdot\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{s}\asymp 1,

where the first
\asymp
follows from (
B.66
).

By Lemma
B.5
,
\widehat{\lambda}_{s}\asymp\lambda_{s}
for
s=1,\ldots,\ell
, which are larger than
\omega
with a probability approaching one. Thus, for
s=1,\ldots,\ell-1
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{s+1}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{s}}I(\widehat{\lambda}_{s}\geq\omega)+I(\widehat{\lambda}_{s}<\omega)\asymp 1.

For
s=\ell+1,\ldots,d_{\max}
, by Lemma
B.5
,
\widehat{\lambda}_{s}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{LN}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{L\wedge N\wedge T}\right)
, which is less than
\omega
with a probability approaching one. Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{s+1}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{s}}I(\widehat{\lambda}_{s}\geq\omega)+I(\widehat{\lambda}_{s}<\omega)=1

for
s=\ell+1,\ldots,d_{\max}
by construction. In addition, for
s=\ell
, it is straightforward to obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{\ell+1}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{\ell}}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{LN}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{L\wedge N\wedge T}\right)

using the facts that
\widehat{\lambda}_{\ell}\asymp 1
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{\ell+1}=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{LN}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\centerdot\centerdot}-\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{\centerdot\centerdot}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{L\wedge N\wedge T}\right)
. Thus, we are ready to conclude that
P(\widehat{\ell}=\ell)\to 1
.

Step 2. Note that
i
and
j
are symmetric. Thus, without loss of generality, we consider two cases: (i) there is at least one
\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}<\ell_{i}^{\circ}
in
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}
, and (ii) there is at least one
\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}>\ell_{i}^{\circ}
in
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}
. Note that case (i) does not rule out the possibility that other estimated numbers of factors may be larger than the true value. Similarly, case (ii) does not rule out the possibility that other estimated numbers of factors may be less than the true value. If we can rule out both cases with a probability approaching one, then
\Pr(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\circ},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\bullet}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bullet}|\widehat{\ell}=\ell)\to 1
.

We now consider case (i), and suppose that
\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}<\ell_{i}^{\circ}
. By Lemma
B.6
, we can show that

\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{i,\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}+1}^{\circ}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{i,\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}}^{\circ}}I(\widehat{\lambda}_{i,\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}}^{\circ}\geq\omega)+I(\widehat{\lambda}_{i,\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}}^{\circ}<\omega)\asymp 1.

By replacing
\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}
of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}
with
\ell_{i}^{\circ}
, we find another
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{S}=(\widehat{\ell}_{1}^{\circ},\ldots,\widehat{\ell}_{i-1}^{\circ},\ell_{i}^{\circ},\widehat{\ell}_{i+1}^{\circ},\ldots\widehat{\ell}_{L}^{\circ})
, which yields a smaller value for the objective function considered in (
2.16
) with a probability approaching one. However, this is contradictory to the definition of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}
.

Next, we consider case (ii), and suppose that
\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}>\ell_{i}^{\circ}
. Again, Lemma
B.6
yields that

\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{i,\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}+1}^{\circ}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{i,\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}}^{\circ}}I(\widehat{\lambda}_{i,\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}}^{\circ}\geq\omega)+I(\widehat{\lambda}_{i,\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}}^{\circ}<\omega)=1.

By replacing
\widehat{\ell}_{i}^{\circ}
of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}
with
\ell_{i}^{\circ}
, we find another
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}=(\widehat{\ell}_{1}^{\circ},\ldots,\widehat{\ell}_{i-1}^{S},\ell_{i}^{\circ},\widehat{\ell}_{i+1}^{\circ},\ldots\widehat{\ell}_{L}^{\circ})
, which yields a smaller value for the objective function considered in (
2.16
) with a probability approaching one. However, it is contradictory to the definition of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}
. Based on the above development, we conclude that
\Pr(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\circ}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\circ},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\bullet}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\bullet}|\widehat{\ell}=\ell)\to 1
.

In view of Steps 1 and 2, the proof is now completed.
\blacksquare
